## Implementation

```python
def solve(n):
    MOD = 1000000007
    
    if n < 2:
        return 0
    if n == 2:
        return 1
    if n == 3:
        return 2
    
    # For n >= 4: 2^(n-2) + (n-3) * 2^(n-4)
    # = 2^(n-4) * (4 + (n-3)) = 2^(n-4) * (n+1)
    
    # Calculate 2^(n-4) mod MOD
    power = pow(2, n-4, MOD)
    result = (power * (n + 1)) % MOD
    return result

T = int(input())
for _ in range(T):
    N = int(input())
    print(solve(N))
```

Wait, let me verify the simplification:
- 2^(n-2) + (n-3) * 2^(n-4)
- = 2^(n-4) * (2^2 + (n-3)) 
- = 2^(n-4) * (4 + n - 3)
- = 2^(n-4) * (n + 1)

Yes! Perfect.

But for n=3, this would be 2^(-1) * 4 which is not valid.

So:
- n=1: 0
- n=2: 1  
- n=3: 2
- n>=4: 2^(n-4) * (n+1)

```python
MOD = 1000000007

T = int(input())
for _ in range(T):
    N = int(input())
    if N < 2:
        print(0)
    elif N == 2:
        print(1)
    elif N == 3:
        print(2)
    else:  # N >= 4
        power = pow(2, N-4, MOD)
        result = (power * (N + 1)) % MOD
        print(result)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A binary string of length N is a string of N characters, where each character is either 0 or 1. 

Wet Shark makes a list binStrings, consisting of all 2^N N-digit binary strings. For each string X in binStrings, Wet Shark runs a function ZeroShark(X), defined as follows (psuedocode):

bool ZeroShark(x):
//note the zero indexing
n = length(x)
counter = 0
FOR i = 1 to n-1
    IF x[i] == '0' and x[i - 1] == '0'
        INCREMENT counter
        END IF
ENDFOR
return (counter == 1)

Wet Shark stores an element X in a new list, binTrues  if ZeroShark(X) returns True for that X . In otherwords, binTrues consists of all the elements X in binStrings such that ZeroShark(X) returns True.

Given T testcases, where each testcase consists of integer N, calculate the length of binTrues for each N. Because the lists can get large, output your answer modulo 10^9+7=1000000007

INPUT

The first line of the input contains a single integer T, the number of such N.

The next T lines of the input contain a single integer N, as described in the problem.

OUTPUT

Output T separate lines, each containing the number of elements in the resulting list binTrues for each N in the input. The lists may be large, so output the answer modulo 10^9+7=1000000007

CONSTRAINTS

1 ≤ T ≤ 10

1 ≤ N ≤ 10^5

SAMPLE INPUT
2

2

3

SAMPLE OUTPUT
1

2

Explanation

When N=2, the list binStrings is equal to ['00', '01', '10', '11'].

Calling ZeroShark on each element in binStrings, the function only returns true when X is equal to 00. When X = 01, 10, 11, the counter in ZeroShark is equal to 0. 

Therefore, binTrues is equal to ['00'], and has a length of 1.

When N=3, the list binStrings is equal to ['000', '001', '010', '100', '011', '101', '110', '111'].

Calling ZeroShark on each element in binStrings, the function only returns true when X is equal to 001 or 100. 

Therefore, binTrues is equal to ['001', '100'], and has a length of 2.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

```python
T = int(input())
MOD = 1000000007

for _ in range(T):
    N = int(input())
    if N == 1:
        print(0)
    else:
        print((N - 1) % MOD)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

One day Vasya was on a physics practical, performing the task on measuring the capacitance. He followed the teacher's advice and did as much as n measurements, and recorded the results in the notebook. After that he was about to show the results to the teacher, but he remembered that at the last lesson, the teacher had made his friend Petya redo the experiment because the largest and the smallest results differed by more than two times. Vasya is lazy, and he does not want to redo the experiment. He wants to do the task and go home play computer games. So he decided to cheat: before Vasya shows the measurements to the teacher, he will erase some of them, so as to make the largest and the smallest results of the remaining measurements differ in no more than two times. In other words, if the remaining measurements have the smallest result x, and the largest result y, then the inequality y ≤ 2·x must fulfill. Of course, to avoid the teacher's suspicion, Vasya wants to remove as few measurement results as possible from his notes.

Help Vasya, find what minimum number of measurement results he will have to erase from his notes so that the largest and the smallest of the remaining results of the measurements differed in no more than two times.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5) — the number of measurements Vasya made. The second line contains n integers c_1, c_2, ..., c_{n} (1 ≤ c_{i} ≤ 5000) — the results of the measurements. The numbers on the second line are separated by single spaces.


-----Output-----

Print a single integer — the minimum number of results Vasya will have to remove.


-----Examples-----
Input
6
4 5 3 8 3 7

Output
2

Input
4
4 3 2 4

Output
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample you can remove the fourth and the sixth measurement results (values 8 and 7). Then the maximum of the remaining values will be 5, and the minimum one will be 3. Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout


Now solve the problem and return the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value is at most 2 times the smallest remaining value
3. That means if the smallest remaining value is `x`, then the largest remaining value must be `≤ 2x`

## Key Insights

1. Since we want to minimize the number of removals, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible
2. The constraint `max ≤ 2 × min` means we need to find a valid subsequence where the ratio between max and min is at most 2
3. We can sort the array first to make it easier to work with
4. After sorting, we can use sliding window technique to find the longest subsequence where `max / min ≤ 2`

## Approach

1. Sort the measurements in ascending order
2. For each possible starting position, find the longest valid subsequence ending at some position where `arr[end] ≤ 2 × arr[start]`
3. The answer will be `n - (length of longest valid subsequence)`

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Sort the array**: This helps us easily find valid subsequences
2. **Use sliding window**: For each starting position, extend the window as long as the ratio condition is satisfied
3. **Track maximum length**: Keep track of the longest valid subsequence
4. **Calculate removals**: Remove the difference between total and maximum valid subsequence length

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements to make it easier to find valid subsequences
measurements.sort()

max_remaining = 0

# For each possible starting position
for i in range(n):
    # Find the farthest position where the ratio condition is satisfied
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    
    # The valid subsequence from i to j-1 has length (j - i)
    current_length = j - i
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, current_length)

# The minimum number of removals is total - maximum valid subsequence length
print(n - max_remaining)
```

## Verification with examples

**Example 1**: `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Starting at 0 (value 3): 3 ≤ 2×3, 3 ≤ 2×3, 4 ≤ 2×3, 5 ≤ 2×3, 7 > 2×3 ✓ → length 4
- Starting at 1 (value 3): 3 ≤ 2×3, 4 ≤ 2×3, 5 ≤ 2×3, 7 > 2×3 ✓ → length 4  
- Starting at 2 (value 4): 4 ≤ 2×4, 5 ≤ 2×4, 7 ≤ 2×4, 8 > 2×4 ✓ → length 4
- Starting at 3 (value 5): 5 ≤ 2×5, 7 ≤ 2×5, 8 > 2×5 ✓ → length 3
- Starting at 4 (value 7): 7 ≤ 2×7, 8 > 2×7 ✓ → length 2
- Starting at 5 (value 8): 8 ≤ 2×8 → length 1
- Maximum length = 4
- Removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2**: `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- Sorted: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- Starting at 0 (value 2): 2 ≤ 2×2, 3 ≤ 2×2, 4 ≤ 2×2? 4 ≤ 4 ✓ → length 4
- All other starting positions give shorter sequences
- Maximum length = 4
- Removals = 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

The solution works correctly!

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of kept measurements)

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can consider all possible subarrays where the maximum element ≤ 2 × minimum element
2. For a sorted array, if we keep a subarray from index i to j, the condition becomes: c[j] ≤ 2 × c[i]
3. We want to find the longest such subarray to minimize removals
4. The answer will be: n - (length of longest valid subarray)

## Solution Approach

1. Sort the measurements
2. Use a sliding window approach to find the longest subarray where max ≤ 2 × min
3. For each starting position, extend the window as far as possible while maintaining the condition
4. Track the maximum window size found
5. Answer = n - maximum_window_size

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

# If we have only 2 elements, check if they satisfy the condition
if n == 2:
    if measurements[1] <= 2 * measurements[0]:
        print(0)
    else:
        print(1)
else:
    max_keep = 1  # At least we can keep one element
    
    # Try all possible starting positions
    for i in range(n):
        # For each starting position, extend as far as possible
        j = i
        while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
            j += 1
        # The valid subarray is from i to j-1
        window_size = j - i
        max_keep = max(max_keep, window_size)
    
    print(n - max_keep)
```

Wait, let me verify this with the examples:

**Example 1:** `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Starting at 0: [3] → 3≤2×3 ✓, [3,3] → 3≤2×3 ✓, [3,3,4] → 4≤2×3 ✓, [3,3,4,5] → 5≤2×3 ✓, [3,3,4,5,7] → 7≤2×3? 7≤6? ❌
- So valid subarray of length 4: [3,3,4,5]
- Starting at 1: [3] → 3≤2×3 ✓, [3,4] → 4≤2×3 ✓, [3,4,5] → 5≤2×3 ✓, [3,4,5,7] → 7≤2×3? ❌
- Valid length 4
- Starting at 2: [4] → 4≤2×4 ✓, [4,5] → 5≤2×4 ✓, [4,5,7] → 7≤2×4? 7≤8 ✓, [4,5,7,8] → 8≤2×4? 8≤8 ✓
- Valid length 4
- Starting at 3: [5] → 5≤2×5 ✓, [5,7] → 7≤2×5? 7≤10 ✓, [5,7,8] → 8≤2×5? 8≤10 ✓
- Valid length 3
- Starting at 4: [7] → 7≤2×7 ✓, [7,8] → 8≤2×7? 8≤14 ✓
- Valid length 2
- Starting at 5: [8] → 8≤2×8 ✓
- Valid length 1

Maximum window size = 4, so removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2:** `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- Sorted: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- Starting at 0: [2,3,4,4] → 4≤2×2? 4≤4 ✓
- Valid length 4, so removals = 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

The solution looks correct!

After that he was about to show the results to the teacher, but he remembered that at the last lesson, the teacher had made his friend Petya redo the experiment because the largest and the smallest results differed by more than two times. Vasya is lazy, and he does not want to redo the experiment. He wants to do the task and go home play computer games. So he decided to cheat: before Vasya shows the measurements to the teacher, he will erase some of them, so as to make the largest and the smallest results of the remaining measurements differ in no more than two times. In other words, if the remaining measurements have the smallest result x, and the largest result y, then the inequality y ≤ 2·x must fulfill. Of course, to avoid the teacher's suspicion, Vasya wants to remove as few measurement results as possible from his notes.

Help Vasya, find what minimum number of measurement results he will have to erase from his notes so that the largest and the smallest of the remaining results of the measurements differed in no more than two times.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5) — the number of measurements Vasya made. The second line contains n integers c_1, c_2, ..., c_{n} (1 ≤ c_{i} ≤ 5000) — the results of the measurements. The numbers on the second line are separated by single spaces.


-----Output-----

Print a single integer — the minimum number of results Vasya will have to remove.


-----Examples-----
Input
6
4 5 3 8 3 7

Output
2

Input
4
4 3 2 4

Output
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample you can remove the fourth and the sixth measurement results (values 8 and 7). Then the maximum of the remaining values will be 5, and the minimum one will be 3. Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Approach

Since we want to minimize removals, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible. The key insight is that we can sort the array and then consider all possible contiguous subarrays where the maximum ≤ 2 × minimum.

Steps:
1. Sort the measurements in ascending order
2. For each possible starting position, find the longest contiguous subarray where the last element ≤ 2 × first element
3. The answer will be n minus the length of the longest such subarray

## Algorithm Details

After sorting:
- For each starting index i, we can extend the subarray to the right as long as `arr[j] ≤ 2 * arr[i]`
- We need to find the maximum length of such a contiguous subarray
- The minimum removals = n - maximum_length

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read input
2. Sort the array
3. Use two pointers (or linear scanning) to find the longest valid contiguous subarray
4. Return n minus the length of that subarray

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

# Find the longest contiguous subarray where max <= 2 * min
max_remaining = 0

# For each possible starting position
for start in range(n):
    # Extend as far as possible from this start position
    end = start
    while end < n and measurements[end] <= 2 * measurements[start]:
        end += 1
    
    # The length of valid subarray from start to end-1
    valid_length = end - start
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, valid_length)

# Minimum removals = total - maximum remaining
print(n - max_remaining)
```

## Verification with Examples

**Example 1:** `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- After sorting: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Start at 0 (value 3): 3,3,4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 2×3=6? No, so valid until 3 → length 3
- Start at 1 (value 3): 3,4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 6? No, so valid until 3 → length 3  
- Start at 2 (value 4): 4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 8? Yes, so 4,5,7 → length 3
- Start at 3 (value 5): 5,7 → 7 ≤ 10? Yes, so 5,7 → length 2
- Start at 4 (value 7): 7 → length 1
- Start at 5 (value 8): 8 → length 1
- Maximum length = 3
- Removals = 6 - 3 = 3

Wait, this doesn't match the expected output of 2. Let me reconsider.

Actually, let me check the condition again. The largest should be ≤ 2 × smallest.
For [3,3,4,5,7,8]:
- [3,3,4,5,7]: 7 ≤ 2×3=6? No
- [3,3,4,5]: 5 ≤ 6? Yes, length 4
- [3,3,4]: 4 ≤ 6? Yes, length 3
- [4,5,7]: 7 ≤ 8? Yes, length 3

I see the issue. Let me trace through the valid subarrays:
- Start at 0: 3,3,4,5 → 5 ≤ 6? Yes, so length 4 (indices 0,1,2,3)
- Start at 1: 3,4,5 → 5 ≤ 6? Yes, so length 3 (indices 1,2,3)
- Start at 2: 4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 8? Yes, so length 3 (indices 2,3,4)
- Start at 3: 5,7 → 7 ≤ 10? Yes, so length 2 (indices 3,4)
- Start at 4: 7 → length 1
- Start at 5: 8 → length 1

Maximum length = 4, removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

The algorithm is correct!

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Key Insights

1. After removing some measurements, if the smallest value is x and largest is y, then y ≤ 2x
2. Since we want to remove as few as possible, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible
3. The optimal solution will involve keeping a contiguous subsequence when the array is sorted

## Algorithm

1. Sort the measurements
2. For each possible starting position, try to extend the sequence as far as possible while maintaining the constraint y ≤ 2x
3. For each starting position i, find the maximum j such that c[j] ≤ 2 × c[i]
4. The number of measurements we keep is (j - i + 1)
5. The number of measurements we remove is (n - (j - i + 1))
6. Take the minimum removals across all possible starting positions

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Sort the array
2. For each possible starting position, find the ending position that satisfies the constraint
3. Track the maximum number of measurements we can keep
4. Return n - maximum_keep

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 0

# Try each possible starting position
for i in range(n):
    # Find the farthest position j where measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    # j is now the first position that violates the constraint
    # so we can keep from i to j-1
    keep_count = j - i
    max_keep = max(max_keep, keep_count)

# Minimum removals = total - max keep
print(n - max_keep)
```

## Example Walkthrough

**Example 1:** [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]
- Sorted: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- Start at 0 (value 3): 3, 3, 4, 5, 7 → 7 > 2×3=6, so stop at 5. Keep 5 measurements (indices 0-4)
- Start at 1 (value 3): 3, 4, 5, 7 → 7 > 2×3=6, so stop at 5. Keep 4 measurements (indices 1-4)
- Start at 2 (value 4): 4, 5, 7 → 7 > 2×4=8? No, 7 ≤ 8. Keep 3 measurements (indices 2-4)
- Start at 3 (value 5): 5, 7 → 7 ≤ 10, keep 2 measurements (indices 3-4)
- Start at 4 (value 7): 7 → keep 1 measurement
- Start at 5 (value 8): keep 1 measurement

Maximum keep = 5, so removals = 6 - 5 = 1? Wait, let me recalculate.

Actually, let me check: for start at 0: values 3,3,4,5 → 5 ≤ 6, so we can keep 4 values. Next is 7 which is > 6, so stop. Keep count = 4-0 = 4.

For start at 2 (value 4): values 4,5,7 → 7 > 8? No, 7 ≤ 8, so keep 3 values.

Actually: 7 ≤ 2×4 = 8, so 7 ≤ 8 is true. So we keep 3 values (indices 2,3,4).

Wait, let me recalculate the constraint: after sorting [3,3,4,5,7,8]
- Start at 0: 3,3,4,5 → 5 ≤ 6? Yes. 7 ≤ 6? No. So keep 4 values (indices 0-3)
- Start at 1: 3,4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 6? No. So keep 3 values (indices 1-3)
- Start at 2: 4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 8? Yes. So keep 3 values (indices 2-4)
- Start at 3: 5,7 → 7 ≤ 10? Yes. So keep 2 values (indices 3-4)
- Start at 4: 7 → keep 1 value
- Start at 5: 8 → keep 1 value

Maximum keep = 4, so removals = 6 - 4 = 2. This matches the expected output.

The algorithm is correct.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can consider all possible contiguous subarrays where the maximum value ≤ 2 × minimum value
2. For a sorted array, if we take a contiguous subarray from index i to j, the condition is: arr[j] ≤ 2 × arr[i]
3. We want to find the longest such contiguous subarray to minimize removals
4. The number of removals = n - (length of longest valid contiguous subarray)

## Algorithm Steps

1. Sort the array of measurements
2. Use two pointers (left and right) to find the longest contiguous subarray where arr[right] ≤ 2 × arr[left]
3. For each possible left position, extend the right pointer as far as possible while maintaining the condition
4. Track the maximum length found
5. Answer = n - maximum_length

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_remaining = 1  # At minimum, we can keep 1 measurement

# Try all possible left boundaries
for left in range(n):
    # Extend right boundary as far as possible from this left
    right = left
    while right < n and measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]:
        right += 1
    
    # The valid subarray is from left to right-1
    length = right - left
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, length)

# Number of measurements to remove = total - remaining
print(n - max_remaining)
```

## Step-by-step trace for examples:

**Example 1:** [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]
- After sorting: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- left=0: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 7 > 2×3 ✗ → length=4 (indices 0-3: [3,3,4,5])
- left=1: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓, 7 > 2×3 ✗ → length=3 (indices 1-3: [3,4,5])
- left=2: 4 ≤ 2×4 ✓, 5 ≤ 2×4 ✓, 7 > 2×4 ✗ → length=2 (indices 2-3: [4,5])
- left=3: 5 ≤ 2×5 ✓, 7 > 2×5 ✗ → length=1 (index 3: [5])
- left=4: 7 ≤ 2×7 ✓, 8 > 2×7 ✗ → length=1 (index 4: [7])
- left=5: 8 ≤ 2×8 ✓ → length=1 (index 5: [8])
- Max length = 4, removals = 6-4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2:** [4, 3, 2, 4]
- After sorting: [2, 3, 4, 4]
- left=0: 2 ≤ 4 ✓, 3 ≤ 4 ✓, 4 ≤ 4 ✓, 4 ≤ 4 ✓ → length=4 (all elements)
- Max length = 4, removals = 4-4 = 0 ✓

The solution works correctly for both examples.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We want to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We need to find the minimum number of measurements to remove

## Key Insights

1. The condition is: `max(remaining) ≤ 2 × min(remaining)`
2. Since we want to minimize removals, we want to maximize the number of measurements we keep
3. The optimal solution will involve keeping a contiguous subsequence of sorted values (since we can only remove elements, not reorder them)

## Strategy

1. Sort the measurements
2. For each possible starting point and ending point, check if the condition holds
3. Find the longest valid subsequence (which means minimum removals)
4. The answer will be `n - length_of_longest_valid_subsequence`

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Sort the array** - this allows us to consider contiguous subsequences
2. **Use two pointers** - for each possible starting position, extend the ending position until the condition breaks
3. **Track the maximum valid length** - this gives us the maximum number of measurements we can keep
4. **Answer** = `n - max_valid_length`

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_kept = 0

# Try all possible subarrays (contiguous in sorted array)
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(i, n):
        # Check if measurements[i:j+1] satisfies the condition
        min_val = measurements[i]
        max_val = measurements[j]
        
        if max_val <= 2 * min_val:
            max_kept = max(max_kept, j - i + 1)

# Minimum removals = total measurements - maximum measurements we can keep
print(n - max_kept)
```

Wait, this O(n²) solution might be too slow for n = 10^5. Let me optimize it.

## Optimized Approach

Since the array is sorted, for each starting position, we can find the maximum ending position efficiently:

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

measurements.sort()

max_kept = 0

# For each starting position
for i in range(n):
    # Find the maximum ending position such that measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    # j is now the first position where condition breaks
    # So valid subsequence from i to j-1 has length (j-1-i+1) = j-i
    max_kept = max(max_kept, j - i)

print(n - max_kept)
```

Let me verify with the examples:

**Example 1**: [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7] → sorted: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- i=0 (3): 3, 3, 4, 5, 7 → 7 > 2*3=6, so j=5. Length = 5-0 = 5 (values: 3,3,4,5)
- Wait, 7 > 6, so should stop at 5? Let me check: 3,3,4,5 → max=5, min=3, 5 ≤ 6 ✓. So length = 4
- i=1 (3): 3,4,5,7 → 7 > 6, so j=5. Length = 5-1 = 4 (values: 3,4,5)
- i=2 (4): 4,5,7 → 7 > 8? 7 ≤ 8 ✓, 7 ≤ 8 ✓, 7 ≤ 8 ✓, so j=6. Length = 6-2 = 4 (values: 4,5,7)
- i=3 (5): 5,7 → 7 ≤ 10 ✓, so j=6. Length = 6-3 = 3
- i=4 (7): 7,8 → 8 ≤ 14 ✓, so j=6. Length = 6-4 = 2
- i=5 (8): 8 → length = 1

Maximum kept = 4, so removals = 6-4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2**: [4, 3, 2, 4] → sorted: [2, 3, 4, 4]
- i=0 (2): 2,3,4 → 4 ≤ 4 ✓, so j=4. Length = 4-0 = 4
- Maximum kept = 4, removals = 4-4 = 0 ✓

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

measurements.sort()

max_kept = 0

# For each starting position
for i in range(n):
    # Find the maximum ending position such that measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    # The valid subsequence is from i to j-1
    max_kept = max(max_kept, j - i)

print(n - max_kept)
```

for i in range(n):
    # Find the maximum ending position such that measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    # The valid subsequence is from i to j-1
    max_kept = max(max_kept, j - i)

print(n - max_kept)

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

One day Vasya was on a physics practical, performing the task on measuring the capacitance. He followed the teacher's advice and did as much as n measurements, and recorded the results in the notebook. After that he was about to show the results to the teacher, but he remembered that at the last lesson, the teacher had made his friend Petya redo the experiment because the largest and the smallest results differed by more than two times. Vasya is lazy, and he does not want to redo the experiment. He wants to do the task and go home play computer games. So he decided to cheat: before Vasya shows the measurements to the teacher, he will erase some of them, so as to make the largest and the smallest results of the remaining measurements differ in no more than two times. In other words, if the remaining measurements have the smallest result x, and the largest result y, then the inequality y ≤ 2·x must fulfill. Of course, to avoid the teacher's suspicion, Vasya wants to remove as few measurement results as possible from his notes.

Help Vasya, find what minimum number of measurement results he will have to erase from his notes so that the largest and the smallest of the remaining results of the measurements differed in no more than two times.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5) — the number of measurements Vasya made. The second line contains n integers c_1, c_2, ..., c_{n} (1 ≤ c_{i} ≤ 5000) — the results of the measurements. The numbers on the second line are separated by single spaces.


-----Output-----

Print a single integer — the minimum number of results Vasya will have to remove.


-----Examples-----
Input
6
4 5 3 8 3 7

Output
2

Input
4
4 3 2 4

Output
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample you can remove the fourth and the sixth measurement results (values 8 and 7). Then the maximum of the remaining values will be 5, and the minimum one will be 3. Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We want to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × (smallest remaining value)
3. We need to find the minimum number of measurements to remove

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can consider all possible contiguous subarrays where the maximum value ≤ 2 × minimum value
2. For any valid subarray after sorting, if the minimum value is x, then the maximum value must be ≤ 2x
3. We want to maximize the size of the remaining array (minimize removals), so we want the longest valid contiguous subarray
4. The answer will be: n - (length of longest valid contiguous subarray)

## Algorithm Steps

1. Sort the array of measurements
2. Use a sliding window approach to find the longest contiguous subarray where max ≤ 2 × min
3. For each starting position, extend the window as long as the condition holds
4. Track the maximum window size
5. Return n - max_window_size

## Implementation Strategy

For each possible starting position i in the sorted array:
- Start with window [i, i] (single element)
- Extend the window to the right while the condition max ≤ 2 × min holds
- The condition is: arr[j] ≤ 2 × arr[i] (since arr[i] is the minimum in the current window)

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements to make it easier to work with contiguous subarrays
measurements.sort()

max_remaining = 0

# Try each possible starting position
for i in range(n):
    # For each starting position, extend the window as much as possible
    j = i
    while j < n:
        # Check if current element exceeds 2 times the minimum (which is measurements[i])
        if measurements[j] > 2 * measurements[i]:
            break
        j += 1
    
    # The valid window is from i to j-1, so its length is (j - i)
    window_length = j - i
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, window_length)

# The minimum number of removals is total measurements minus the maximum remaining
print(n - max_remaining)
```

Wait, let me trace through the first example:
- Input: [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]
- After sorting: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- For i=0 (min=3): we need max ≤ 6. Elements 3,3,4,5 are ≤ 6. So window [0,3] has length 4
- For i=1 (min=3): we need max ≤ 6. Elements 3,4,5 are ≤ 6. So window [1,3] has length 3  
- For i=2 (min=4): we need max ≤ 8. Elements 4,5,7 are ≤ 8. So window [2,4] has length 3
- For i=3 (min=5): we need max ≤ 10. Elements 5,7,8 are ≤ 10. So window [3,5] has length 3
- For i=4 (min=7): we need max ≤ 14. Only element 7. Window [4,4] has length 1
- For i=5 (min=8): only element 8. Window [5,5] has length 1

Maximum window length = 4, so removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

This matches the expected output.

Let me also check the second example:
- Input: [4, 3, 2, 4]
- After sorting: [2, 3, 4, 4]
- For i=0 (min=2): max ≤ 4. Elements 2,3,4 are ≤ 4. Window [0,2] has length 3
- For i=1 (min=3): max ≤ 6. Elements 3,4,4 are ≤ 6. Window [1,3] has length 3
- For i=2 (min=4): max ≤ 8. Elements 4,4 are ≤ 8. Window [2,3] has length 2
- For i=3 (min=4): only element 4. Window [3,3] has length 1

Maximum window length = 3, so removals = 4 - 3 = 1

Wait, this gives 1 but expected is 0. Let me reconsider.

Actually, looking at the example: [4,3,2,4]. After sorting: [2,3,4,4]
With all elements: min=2, max=4, and 4 ≤ 2×2 = 4, so the condition holds! So we don't need to remove any.

I made a mistake in my analysis. The condition is satisfied with the entire array. So the maximum window length is 4, not 3.

Let me fix the algorithm - I need to check if the condition holds for each window.

```python

```

- i=0: min=2, check: 2≤4✓, 3≤4✓, 4≤4✓, 4≤4✓. j becomes 4, window length = 4
- So max_remaining = 4, removals = 4-4 = 0 ✓

Perfect! The algorithm works correctly.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (which means maximizing the number of measurements we keep)

## Key Insights

1. Since we want to minimize removals, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible
2. The constraint is: `max(remaining) ≤ 2 × min(remaining)`
3. After sorting the array, we can use a sliding window approach to find the longest subsequence that satisfies the condition

## Strategy

1. Sort the measurements in ascending order
2. Use a sliding window approach where we try all possible subarrays
3. For each subarray, check if the last element ≤ 2 × first element
4. The length of the longest valid subarray gives us the maximum measurements we can keep
5. Answer = `n - (length of longest valid subarray)`

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Sort the array**: This allows us to easily work with the smallest and largest elements in any subarray
2. **Sliding window**: For each starting position, extend the window as long as the condition `arr[end] ≤ 2 × arr[start]` holds
3. **Track maximum valid length**: Keep track of the longest subarray that satisfies the condition
4. **Calculate removals**: Subtract the maximum valid length from total measurements

## Example walkthrough

For input `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`:
- After sorting: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Check subarrays:
  - `[3, 3]`: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[3, 4]`: 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[3, 5]`: 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[3, 7]`: 7 ≤ 2×3? 7 ≤ 6? ✗
  - `[3, 8]`: 8 ≤ 6? ✗
  - `[4, 5]`: 5 ≤ 8 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[4, 7]`: 7 ≤ 8 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[4, 8]`: 8 ≤ 8 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[5, 7]`: 7 ≤ 10 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[5, 8]`: 8 ≤ 10 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[7, 8]`: 8 ≤ 14 ✓ (length 2)
- Longest valid subarray has length 2
- Removals needed = 6 - 2 = 4

Wait, let me reconsider. Looking at the sample output, answer is 2, so we should keep 4 measurements.

Let me recheck the condition. Actually, if we keep `[3, 4, 5, 7]`:
- min = 3, max = 7
- 7 ≤ 2×3? 7 ≤ 6? No.

What about `[4, 5, 7, 8]`:
- min = 4, max = 8
- 8 ≤ 2×4? 8 ≤ 8? Yes!

This has length 4, so removals = 6 - 4 = 2. This matches.

So the correct approach is:
- Sort the array
- For each possible starting position, find the maximum ending position such that `arr[end] ≤ 2 × arr[start]`
- Keep track of the maximum length found

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 0

# Try each starting position
for i in range(n):
    # For each starting position, extend as far as possible
    j = i
    while j < n and measurements[j] <= 2 * measurements[i]:
        j += 1
    # j is now the first position where condition fails
    # so valid range is from i to j-1
    valid_length = j - i
    max_keep = max(max_keep, valid_length)

# Number of removals = total - maximum we can keep
print(n - max_keep)
```
